# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド39・40（レッスン 1141〜1200）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校・中学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 1141 --to 1200`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 51 / info 11 → あと error 0 / warn 21 / info 12**（のこりの warn は ぜんぶ わざと: テストで きまった タイトルの「…」〈However…・It Seems That…・We Believe That…・Our First Point Is…・We Disagree Because…・One Solution Is…・I Hope to…〉と その スライド 13、L1184 の かんがえる 間「Let me see…」6、書きかたの ゆれ「bow and say, Thank you」1）
- なおした 欄: **534**（パッチ 574 件。ブロックの きめごと 131・AI の さいしょの ひとこと 60・レビュアー 373・あとの そろえ 10）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で、HEAD から じゅんばんに あてなおした（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあい・かいわ あいて（partner.ts）・文法の じゅんばん・きんしの ことば・団体や 試験の なまえ（order.mjs。テストが 見ない teacher.segments も）を たしかめた（ALL OK）

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. イギリスふうの ほめことば: 「Brilliant!」→「Great job!」（L1145・L1150・L1152・L1161・L1170・L1177・L1178・L1184）、「Lovely!」→「Wonderful! / Nice job!」（L1168・L1172・L1189・L1193・L1194）、L1187 の 会話「Lovely plans.」→「Great plans.」
2. アメリカ英語: L1154・L1155「(the) summer holidays」→「summer vacation」（ことば・表現・会話・スライド ぜんぶ）、L1143「football」→「soccer」、L1173「In Year 1」→「In our first year」、「Okay / okay」→「OK」（L1153・L1160・L1174）
3. エマ先生 じぶんの 語り: say・字幕の「Ms. Emma says / asks」→「I say / I ask」（日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」。L1161・L1162・L1163・L1170）、teacherScript の「Ms. Emma says / smiles / nods / laughs / gives / writes / sits / teaches / helps ～」→ I ～（with everyone / me。36 レッスン）。こどもが エマ先生に はなしかける 文、こどもが 書いた カード・手紙・リスト・スピーチ（「Dear Ms. Emma」「A note from Ms. Emma:」「With love, Ms. Emma」など）と それへの しつもん、会話の 話し手「Ms. Emma」、goalJa の「AI の エマ先生」は のこした
4. エマ先生の あいさつ「Good morning / Good evening, ～!」→「Hi, ～!」（L1144・L1149・L1153・L1157・L1162・L1173・L1193・L1195・L1199 の さいしょ。日本語「やあ、みんな！」）。物語の「Tonight, ～」、面接の ロールプレイの「Good morning. Please sit down.」（L1181・L1185）、スピーチの 出だしの れい「Good morning, EVeryone」（L1186）は のこした
5. **AI の さいしょの ひとこと**: アプリは あいてを こどもの キャラ（ハル・リコ・キコ・ビリモ）から えらび、その かおと なまえで ひとことを 出す。なのに 60 レッスン ぜんぶが オービット・サム・アミナ・プリヤ・マテオ（の おじさん）・サムの おばさん・リアム・ナディア・セレスト・エマ先生（面接官）・はじめたばかりの 子・フェアの お客さん・はんたいチームの 子 として 話して いた。goalJa の やくは のこし、「Let's pretend I'm ～.」で ごっこあそびに した（W25-38 と おなじ。W27-28 にも「Let's pretend I'm Ms. Emma.」が ある）。「I'm Orbit, the chair」「I'm Sam from North America」「I'm Celeste, the keeper of this star」は わくに まとめ、2つめの Let's は「We can / Here are / Now, ～ begins」に（L1196 は core「Let's talk about anything you like!」なので のこした）、L1182 の 23 語の 文を 2つに
6. **話す 人の ない セリフ**: 「Kiko smiles. I think ～」「Riko points. Orbit, ～」のように、うごきの すぐ あとに こどもの セリフが あって、エマ先生の ことばに きこえる 文に「and says,」「Kiko says,」などを つけた（6 グループ ぜんぶで たくさん。say を 中心に、物語の くいちがいに なる ものは teacherScript も）
7. **声に 出す ための なおし**: たんどくの 字の かたまり（ion・ation・al・ar・ed・ow・ai・ph・ea・er・ful・ly・ay・ir・Im）を 字の 読みあげ（i, o, n）か ほんものの ことば（the sound in her）に。ment・able・ness・re・eco など ことばとして 読める ものは のこした。L1199「Make FU strong」→「Make the start of future strong」（FU を 字で 読むと 下品な ことばに きこえる）、「Lead, as in lead the way」「Tear, as in teardrop」など 読みかたの ちがう ことば
8. **however の 強勢**: L1156・L1160 の「HOWever」→「howEVer」（つよいのは 2つめの 音節）と src/lib/curriculum/w39Phonics.ts の say。おなじ 表の せつめい「はじめを つよく」も moreOver・as a reSULT・for exAMple には あわないので「大文字の ところを つよく」に（content の「さいしょを つよく」「Strong start」も）
9. **ランタン・光が ともる 文**: 現在形の 語り（begins to shine / starts to glow）の あとの「The Lantern of ～ lit up!」を「lights up!」に そろえた（teacherScript の 語り 10 レッスン）。こどもが さけぶ 文（L1175 の 表現「The Ray of Memories lit up!」・L1180・L1185 seg9）と エマ先生の「Look! ～ lit up!」は のこした

## 学習項目が かわった もの
- L1154 vocab[2]「summer holidays」→「summer vacation」と school.expressions[1]「Good point, but what about the summer holidays?」→「～ about summer vacation?」（アメリカ英語。テスト・src には 書かれて いない。core ではない）
- L1187 school.expressions[1]「～ study sea animals at university.」→「～ at a university.」（アメリカ英語。core ではない）
- L1156・L1160 phonics.sounds「HOWever, ‖」→「howEVer, ‖」と src/lib/curriculum/w39Phonics.ts の However の say・せつめいの 文（テストは 字だけ 見て いる）
- core 表現・フォニックスの ことば・タイトル・TPR は かえて いない

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・テストで きまって いる・あとで 決める）
- タイトルと スライドの「…」、L1184「Let me see…」（かんがえる 間 そのもの）、会話の「...」
- **L1141 core「Children from every continent sit at this table.」**: 南極の いすは 空いて いる ので 厳密には ちがうが、テストで きまった 表現（tests/curriculum.test.ts:4519）。ほかの 文では「from all over the world」に した（L1177 など）。かえる なら テストと いっしょに
- 「wonky veggie box」（L1147・L1150。アメリカでも つかう ことばで、かわりの「ugly」は テストの きんしの ことば）、「tap」（L1158。アメリカでも わかる）
- teacherScript の なかの 話す 人の ない 文の 一部（声に なるのは say なので、物語の くいちがいが ない ものは のこした）
- say の 大文字の 強勢（We BELIEVE・Six years aGO・EVeryone など）と スピーチの 間の「pause」（W36-38 と おなじ）
- 書きかたの ゆれ「bow and say, Thank you」（L1180・L1191）と「bow and say thank you」（L1181）

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L1141-1150 (world 39, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 36 patches. L1141 has no change. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean after the patches: point of view starts in L1146, however in L1148, it seems in L1149, and moreover / therefore in L1150. No later form (we believe that, we disagree, If everyone ... would, we propose that) shows up, and order.mjs prints nothing. Themes: (1) **Bare word-part letters for TTS**: "add ion" (TTS says eye-on), "add ation", "add al" (the name Al), "plus ar" and "danger, ed" (the name Ed) in the eco-word phonics of L1142-1145. They are now spelled as letters (i, o, n / a, t, i, o, n / a, l / a, r / e, d), as in L901, L902 and L950, in the segments and in the teacherScript. Real-word parts (environ, ment, sustain, able, re, new, eco, sol, de, en) are kept, following the W27-W31 precedent ("plus ment", "plus able"). (2) **Quotes with no "says"** in say (L1142, 1145, 1146, 1147, 1148, 1149, and four in L1150). "Riko laughs. Ha ha!", "Kiko finishes. Sharing ..." and similar lines make the voice read the child's words as mine, so "and says," is added. (3) **US usage**: the "best-before date" in L1147 (British) is now "best-by date". (4) **Story continuity**: in L1149, the teacherScript gave the target line to Riko, but in the conversation and seg7 Haru corrects himself. Facts checked: about one third of food is lost or wasted (L1142, 1145, 1150). "Millions of people" without clean water nearby is an understatement but true, with a trusted-grown-up line. Millions of children out of school is true. No children live in Antarctica. Carrots take about three months. One million has 6 zeros, ten million 7, one hundred million 8, one billion 9. About eight billion people live on Earth. 2.5 million = two and a half million. Turtles and seabirds mistake plastic for food. Survey: 30/40 = three quarters, 20/40 = half and 10/40 = a quarter, all correct. About 70% of the Earth's surface is water. Both lanterns come in the right lessons (L1145 Knowing, L1150 Many Views), and the wrap-up partners are unchanged. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 217-228 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1141 The World Round Table
- A: The cloud stairs, Orbit's welcome and the penguins' hello are a warm opening. seg5 explains both meanings of chair clearly. The core "Children from every continent sit at this table" sits a little oddly next to the empty Antarctica chair, but it is test-pinned, and the story explains the empty chair (single flag, kept).
- B: The continent framing (Amina from Africa ...) matches the brief. The eco word parts are right (environ + ment, sustain + able, re + new + able). The n-before-m tip is useful.
- C: "Environ, plus ment" reads fine (real-word parts, precedent). seg6 runs Birimo's line into my "That's Orbit, the chair." That is my own line, so it is OK.
- Decision: no change.

#### L1142 Food Waste
- A: Birimo's mountain of food and five pancakes, Sam's fact and the fried-rice leftovers are natural. "thrown away at shops" is fine (the brief allows shop). In seg6, "Birimo brings a mountain of food. Oh no, I can't eat another bite." has no "says".
- B: The one-third fact is right. The serving and leftovers exchanges are clear. The phonics rules are correct (pollute - e + ion; conserve - e + ation).
- C: In seg9 / seg10 and the teacherScript, the bare "ion" is read as the word ion, and "ation" is not a word. In seg6 the voice reads Birimo's line as mine.
- Decision: seg6 -> "... and says, Oh no, ..." (A+C). seg9 / seg10 / teacherScript -> "add i, o, n" and "add a, t, i, o, n" (B+C).

#### L1143 Clean Water for Everyone
- A: Amina's letter (the well by the market, her little brother, soccer) is warm and respectful. The "talk to a grown-up you trust" line is there, and goalJa keeps おうちの 人.
- B: Hedged facts are fine. eco = home / nature and de = take away are right. global = globe - e + al is right.
- C: The bare "ation" (seg9, teacherScript) and "al" (seg10, teacherScript) are misread. "De" is kept: it reads as a prefix (single flag). Conversation line 8 JA 先生も よ is Ms. Emma speaking in the scene, so it is kept (L1089 precedent).
- Decision: seg9 / teacherScript -> "a, t, i, o, n"; seg10 / teacherScript -> "a, l" (B+C).

#### L1144 Every Child Can Learn
- A: Priya's library bus (five villages, two books for one week, the girl who wants to be a doctor) is lovely and fair. The blog post is Priya's own.
- B: "Should means it's the right thing" is a fine kid-level gloss. The JA "いける ように なるべき" is a little literal, but it is understandable (single flag, kept). solar / lunar is a nice pair.
- C: In "En, danger, ed", "ed" is read as the name Ed. "Sol, plus ar" reads "are" (seg9 and the teacherScript).
- Decision: seg9 / teacherScript -> "e, d" and "a, r" (B+C).

#### L1145 Our World Map of Challenges
- A: The pin colors, Birimo's bread with a face and the presentation with good news on every card are a strong model. In seg7, "Haru stares at the green pins. They're everywhere." has no "says".
- B: affect is defined simply. "Maybe clean water, because ..." gives a reason. The Lantern of Knowing lights here, as pinned. "Present our map" (seg1) is a heteronym, but it reads as a verb in context (single flag, kept).
- C: The same seg7 speaker flag. In seg10, "Sol, plus ar." has the same "ar" problem as L1144.
- Decision: seg7 -> "... and says, They're everywhere." (A+C). seg10 -> "a, r" (B+C).

#### L1146 From the Farmer's Point of View
- A: The two-legged carrot, the heart carrot and the uncle's interview are fun and true (about three months; some shops want straight carrots). Riko's "No way." answers a yes/no question (an emphatic no). That is natural here, unlike L1089 (kept). In seg6, "Riko laughs. Ha ha! ..." has no "says".
- B: point of view starts here, and the allowed JA hint is in the teacherScript. The big-number rules (6 / 7 / 8 zeros; two million, not two millions) are right.
- C: The same seg6 flag. The numbers are written as words in say.
- Decision: seg6 -> "Riko laughs and says, Ha ha! ..." (A+C).

#### L1147 From the Shop's Point of View
- A: Sam's aunt's shop and the Wonky Veggie Box notice are good. The notice says "best-before date", which is British; US labels say "best by" / "best if used by". In seg7, "Riko nods. Shops throw away ..." has no "says". "wonky" leans British ("wonky veg"), but US speakers know it (single flag; it is a learning item, kept). "grocery shop" is allowed by the brief.
- B: The date-label safety advice (look, smell, ask a grown-up) is right. 1 billion = 9 zeros, and about 8 billion people is right. In the seg9 JA "80 おくにんの 人", 人 appears twice.
- C: The same seg7 flag.
- Decision: teacherScript "best-before" -> "best-by" (twice; objective, US policy). seg7 -> "Riko nods and says, ..." (A+C). seg9 JA -> 80 おく人 (objective, JA).

#### L1148 However…
- A: Liam's bottle, the beach photo, the clean-up report and the crab's thank-you are natural. Grown-ups pick up sharp things, which is safe. In seg6, "Riko laughs. Plastic is handy." has no "says".
- B: however starts here, as the order rule needs. It starts a new sentence and has a comma, everywhere. Half / one third / two thirds (with the s) are taught right. "One part is half" (seg9) is casual, but fine.
- C: The same seg6 flag.
- Decision: seg6 -> "Riko laughs and says, Plastic is handy." (A+C).

#### L1149 It Seems That…
- A: The survey of forty people, Birimo's tall tally chart and his brushing joke are fun. In seg7, "So Haru tries again. It seems that ..." has no "says".
- B: it seems that and might start here. The survey arithmetic is right (30/40, 20/40 = half, 10/40 = a quarter), and so is "only about our group". The teacherScript says "Riko tries it. It seems that ...", but in the conversation (lines 2-5) and seg7, Haru overclaims and then fixes his own sentence.
- C: The same seg7 flag.
- Decision: seg7 -> "... tries again and says, It seems that ..." (A+C). teacherScript "Riko tries it." -> "Haru tries again." (objective, story).

#### L1150 Many Points of View
- A: The four posters (farmer, shop, family, sea), Birimo's noodle pancakes and Kiko's ending are a good review. The Lantern of Many Views lights here, as pinned. In seg6 (twice), seg7 and seg8, "holds the ... poster. From ...", "Kiko giggles. And ..." and "Kiko finishes. Sharing ..." have no "says".
- B: moreover (adds another strong point) and therefore (shows the result) start here and are explained clearly. The presentation also reuses however correctly. "Four: the farmer, the shop, the family and the sea" matches the posters.
- C: The same speaker flags. In seg8 the voice cannot tell where my narration ends and Kiko begins.
- Decision: seg6 / seg7 / seg8 -> "... and says, ..." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Bare suffix letters in W39 eco-word phonics** (L1141-1145, and reviews in L1150, 1155 ...): grep say and the teacherScript for `add ion|add ation|plus ation|add al\b|plus ar\b|, ed\.|plus ed\b|plus ous|plus ive|plus ity` and spell the letters (i, o, n / a, t, i, o, n / a, l). Real-word parts (ment, able, re, new, sol, eco) were kept, following precedent.
- **"X laughs / nods / giggles / finishes / holds ... . <quote>" in say** with no "says" came up 10 times in this group. Other W39 groups probably have it too (grep say for `(laughs|nods|giggles|finishes|smiles|stares at [^.]*|holds [^.]*)\. [A-Z]`). The teacherScript uses the same narrative style ("Orbit laughs. No children live there", "Haru tries it. ...", "Birimo gasps. Oh no!"). I kept it there, following the L1081-1090 example, which fixed say only.
- **best-before date** -> best-by date (US). Watch for "use-by", "sell-by" in the sharing fridge / sharing shelf lessons (L1156-1165).
- **wonky veggie box** (vocab, TPR, notice) is British-leaning ("wonky veg"). The US equivalent is "ugly / imperfect produce". It is a learning item, and L1150 reuses "wonky veggie boxes", so it is kept. Single flag for the lead.
- Kept on purpose: the test-pinned "Children from every continent sit at this table" next to the empty Antarctica chair; "grocery shop" (brief: shop is fine); "No way." as an emphatic no in L1146; conversation-line JA 先生も よ (Ms. Emma in the scene); "De" / "En" / "Re" prefixes read as syllables; "Present our map" (verb in context); the JA "いける ように なるべき" in L1144.

### Run 9 review notes: L1151-1160 (world 39, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi openings, summer vacation, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 61 patches. L1159 has no change. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics.words are untouched. phonics.sounds[0] of L1156 and L1160 changes from "HOWever, ‖" to "howEVer, ‖". No test reads that field, but src/lib/curriculum/w39Phonics.ts:167 still has `say: "HOWever,"` for the quiz (see Block-wide notes). The grammar order is clean after the patches. order.mjs prints nothing: no we believe that before L1152, no we disagree before L1154, and no If everyone ... would before L1157. Themes: (1) **Wrong stress on however**: "HOWever" teaches the wrong syllable. The word is how-EV-er. The phonics sounds, tips, testPoints, teacherScript and segments of L1156 and L1160 now use howEVer. (2) **"Say the first part strongly"**: the rule was wrong even before the however fix (for exAMple, moreOver, as a reSULT, on the OTHer hand). It is now "the capital / strong part" in L1156, L1157 and L1160 (EN and JA). In L1152, "Strong in the middle" did not fit STRONGly. (3) **Quotes with no "says"** (L1151, L1152, L1154, L1155, L1156, L1158): the child's "I / we" line was read as mine, so "and says / and asks" is added. In L1156 seg7, "No way!" had no speaker in EN, but the JA has リコちゃん. (4) **Story continuity**: in L1151, Birimo's "Rule one: take turns" is rule two on Orbit's sign. In L1157, the JA name is くらい 夜空の 1 時間 instead of くらい 空の 1 時間. (5) **US / leftover policy**: the L1154 JA subtitle still had "summer holidays", and the L1160 checklist used "tick". Facts checked: about one third of food is wasted (L1156). Radishes and lettuce grow fast, so a harvest before summer is plausible (L1155). The 3-3 show of hands is a tie (L1155). Unplugging chargers saves a little energy (L1157). The trusted-grown-up safety line is in L1158 and its goalJa keeps おうちの 人. Twenty children / twenty taps is consistent (L1158). Fifty percent = half, and percent has no s (L1160). The lanterns are right: the third (Debate) lights in L1155 and the fourth (Action) in L1160, and the names stay. The partners in the wrap-ups are unchanged. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 220-228 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1151 What Is a Debate?
- A: Orbit's six-rule sign, "Is it a fight? Not at all!", Birimo's glasses with no glass and Amina's lunch-break debate are natural and warm. Kiko's conversation JA "しつれいに したくないな" is not natural Japanese. The quiz A "You're so silly!" = 「ばかみたい！」 is a little strong in JA (single flag, kept).
- B: The rules are clear and claim-free (we believe that is not used before L1152). In the conversation, Birimo says "Rule one: take turns", but on the sign rule one is two teams and rule two is take turns.
- C: In seg7, "Kiko worries. What if I don't agree? I don't want to be rude." and "Kiko smiles. Got it." have no "says", so the voice reads the lines as mine. The teacherScript Japanese hint "A debate（ディベート）" is the allowed one.
- Decision: line 3 "Rule one" -> "Rule two" with JA ルール 2 (objective, story). Line 4 JA -> しつれいな ことは したくないな (objective JA). seg7 -> "Kiko worries and asks, ..." / "Kiko smiles and says, Got it." (A+C).

#### L1152 We Believe That…
- A: The topic reveal, "you sometimes speak for a side that isn't your own opinion", the claim card and Haru's three tries are a good model. seg16 "Maybe, schools should have more books." uses Maybe as an example marker.
- B: We believe that starts here, as the order rule needs. seg8 "Strong in the middle, and down at the end" does not fit "We STRONGly believe" (the stress is at the start). seg16: "Maybe," reads as hedging, which comes in L1161. The JA says たとえば.
- C: seg6 "Birimo reads the topic. Which side are you on, Haru?" and "Riko stands up tall. We believe that ..." have no "says". In seg7, "Birimo grins. I'm playing ..." is read as me.
- Decision: seg6 / seg7 add "and asks / and says" (A+C). seg8 -> "Strong on the key word" with JA だいじな ことばを (B+C). seg16 -> "For example," (A+B).

#### L1153 Our First Point Is…
- A: "A claim without reasons is like a tree without roots", Mateo's farmer uncle and the beans are nice. In line 7, Kiko (the no team) says "But don't worry, we'll think of an answer!" to the other team. That is odd, and the JA says でも 見てて (just wait).
- B: The first point / second point / example structure and "evidence = facts or examples" are clear. FIRST, SEC-ond and ex-AM-ple are stressed correctly. seg16 has the same "Maybe," flag as L1152.
- C: Reads well. Haru's JA わたしたちの (line 1) vs ぼくたちの (seg6) is a single flag (formal debate voice), kept.
- Decision: line 7 -> "But just wait, we'll think of an answer!" (A+B). seg16 -> "For example," (A+B).

#### L1154 We Disagree Because…
- A: The tired-gardener Birimo, the rebuttal card ("That is a good point ... However, we disagree because ...") and "That's the magic of a debate" are a real classroom model. seg16 "Imagine the topic is, Homework every day." is not a debate topic (no statement to agree or disagree with).
- B: We disagree starts here. "Good point, but what about ~?" is a model rebuttal. The seg4 JA still reads "summer holidays。" although the EN and vocab are summer vacation (left over from the block-wide swap). Same topic flag.
- C: In seg7, "Haru thinks. Maybe families could help." has no "says", so it sounds like my suggestion. "Superb!" reads fine.
- Decision: seg4 JA -> summer vacation (objective, US policy). seg7 -> "Haru thinks and says, ..." (A+C). seg16 -> "Students should have homework every day." with JA to match (A+B).

#### L1155 Mini Debate
- A: The debate script, the tie and "That is the real win!" are lovely. seg1 "Start the yes team" is unnatural. Ms. Emma saying "Beep!" in the conversation while Orbit is the timer is a single flag (she chairs the scene), kept. Mateo voting no after his beans is a single flag, kept.
- B: The review is correct (beLIEVE, disaGREE, half = one of two equal parts, pollute -> pollution). "The Lantern of Debate lit up!" switches to the past inside present narration.
- C: seg8 / teacherScript "drop the e and add ion": TTS reads a bare "ion" as the word ion (eye-on), not the -tion ending. In seg7, "Kiko smiles. Good point! Thank you for a great debate." has no "says".
- Decision: seg1 -> "Start with the yes team" (A+B). seg7 -> "Kiko smiles and says," (A+C). "add ion" -> "add i, o, n" in seg8 and the teacherScript (B+C). "lit up" -> "lights up" (A+C; the lantern name stays for the test).

#### L1156 One Solution Is…
- A: Sam's sharing fridge, the bakery bread, Kiko's sharing shelf and the squirrel joke are good. The fridge notice is realistic (dates, no raw meat or fish, volunteers check). In seg7, "No way! Take what you need" has no speaker.
- B: "HOWever" marks the wrong syllable. "However" is how-EV-er (dictionary stress on the second syllable). "Say the first part strongly" is also wrong for for exAMple. Fact: "about one third of food is wasted" is OK per the brief.
- C: The voice would say how-EV-er, so the caps fight the voice. In seg7, Birimo's "I'll take all the nuts!" and Riko's "No way!" have no speakers, so they read as mine, and the JA has リコちゃん.
- Decision: HOWever -> howEVer in phonics.sounds[0], tipJa, testPoints[1], teacherScript, seg8 and seg9 (HowEVer at the start of a sentence), and "first part" -> "capital / strong part" (EN and JA) (objective). seg7 -> "Birimo plays a hungry squirrel and says, ... Riko says, No way! ..." (objective, speaker / EN-JA).

#### L1157 If Everyone Did It
- A: Nadia's Dark Sky Hour, the poster (busy roads and stations stay lit) and Birimo's sleepy bulb are good. Seeing the Milky Way in a town is a single flag, kept (it is a dark-sky event, and the poster says "many children").
- B: If everyone + past, we would + base verb starts here, as the order rule needs. "Turned and would help us imagine something that isn't happening" is right for kids. seg8 "Strong start, then pause" is wrong for moreOver / as a reSULT / on the OTHer hand. The seg6 JA くらい 夜空の 1 時間 differs from くらい 空の 1 時間 everywhere else.
- C: Same "Strong start" flag. Sentence-initial lowercase "moreOver," in seg8 / seg9 is a single flag, kept (caps only on the stressed part).
- Decision: seg8 -> "Strong part, then pause." with JA 大文字の ところを つよく (B+C). seg6 JA -> くらい 空の 1 時間 (A+B).

#### L1158 Small Actions, Big Changes
- A: Kiko's worry, my "You don't have to fix the whole world" and the trusted-grown-up line are exactly right. Liam's chart and Captain Tap are fun. "tap" is understood in the US (tap water), though "faucet" / "turn off the water" is more common. It is a learning item across vocab, an expression, TPR and Captain Tap, so it is kept (see Block-wide).
- B: In addition / instead / in conclusion are stressed correctly. The twenty children and twenty taps match. goalJa keeps おうちの 人.
- C: In seg7, "Kiko thinks. I could turn off the tap ..." has no "says", so it reads as mine.
- Decision: seg7 -> "Kiko thinks and says, ..." (A+C).

#### L1159 Working Together
- A: The idea clash (Sam, Haru, Kiko, Birimo's leftover robot), Riko's "Why choose only one?" and the make-up handshake are natural.
- B: Combine is well defined. First of all / Finally / Because of this are used correctly on the idea board. "FIRST of all" stresses first, while native speech puts the main stress on ALL. This is a single flag, kept (first is stressed too, and the src table matches).
- C: Reads well. The robot answer in seg7 is mine (Ms. Emma in the conversation). The JA ガリガリ / バリバリ variation is kept.
- Decision: no change.

#### L1160 Action Plan
- A: The three-step plan with the community center, polite letter, posters only where allowed, and grown-ups checking daily is realistic and safe. In seg5, "A list you tick" is British. In the plan, "Therefore, everyone can help, step by step." does not follow from the bread goal before it.
- B: The review "HOWever" has the same wrong-stress problem as L1156. "Strong start, then a pause" is wrong for howEVer. Fifty percent = half, and there is no s on percent: correct. "The Lantern of Action lit up!" has the same tense flag as L1155.
- C: Same however / tense flags. "No s on percent" reads OK.
- Decision: HOWever -> howEVer in phonics.sounds[0], tipJa, testPoints[1], teacherScript, seg8 (with slide item) and seg9, and "Strong start" -> "Stress the capital part" (EN and JA) (objective). seg5 -> "A list you check off ... Check, check, done!" (objective, US policy). "Therefore, everyone can help" -> "With this plan, everyone can help" (A+B). "lit up" -> "lights up" (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **HOWever is wrong stress (how-EV-er)**: src/lib/curriculum/w39Phonics.ts:167 `However: { ..., say: "HOWever,", ...}` still teaches HOW. The quiz `say` should be "howEVer," (tests/phonics-w39.test.ts:79 only checks the letters, so it still passes). Grep all W39 lessons (L1148 However…, L1164, L1166-1170 reviews) and docs for `HOWever`.
- **"Say the first part / Strong start"** for linking words: wrong for howEVer, for exAMple, moreOver, as a reSULT, on the OTHer hand, in adDItion. Grep `first part|Strong start|さいしょを つよく|さいしょの 大文字` in L1148-1150 and L1161-1170.
- **"Maybe," as an example marker** in "Your turn!" segments (before hedging in L1161): use "For example,".
- **Quotes with no "says"** ("X worries. What if I ...", "X grins. I'm playing ...", "X thinks. I could ..."): add "and says / and asks".
- **"lit up!" for the lantern** inside present narration: L1165 / L1170 may have "The Lantern of ~ lit up!" too.
- **"tap"** (L1158, also "A dripping tap" in w39Phonics.ts:168): understood in the US, but faucet / turn off the water is more common. It is a learning item, kept. Decide centrally if it should change.
- Kept on purpose: "FIRST of all" caps (single flag), Ms. Emma's "Beep!" as chair in the L1155 conversation, the Milky Way in L1157, "No s on percent", sentence-initial lowercase "moreOver,", the quiz "You're so silly!" / "Your idea is silly!" (the wrong answers), Birimo's "Hmm, hmm" judge, Orbit "his".

### Run 9 review notes: L1161-1170 (world 39, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 71 patches, and every lesson has at least one. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean after the patches (order.mjs prints nothing). We propose that is still first used in L1163, and the six lantern names, the L1163 sharing shelf, the L1170 star and the speed TPR are unchanged. Themes: (1) **Quotes with no "says" / answers with no speaker** (the biggest group, in all 10 lessons): in "Haru gasps. Only two minutes?", "Kiko begins. Welcome, everyone.", "Birimo is playing Amina. I love your idea." and "What's your opinion? I think safety comes first." the voice reads the child's line as Ms. Emma's own. Now they read "and says," / "X says,", with the JA speaker to match. (2) **Bare letter groups for TTS**, following the L1141-1150 group: "add ation" -> "a, t, i, o, n" (L1167), "add al" -> "a, l" (L1168), "plus ed" -> "e, d" (L1169), in both say and the teacherScript. "En" and "Re" are kept, as in L1141-1150. (3) **Broken frame sentences** read aloud: "Today, we'll talk about, and the topic." (L1162), "Then, This will help, and the good result." (L1163) and "Then, However, and your worry, kindly." (L1164). (4) **Hedging accuracy**: "kinder than always" -> "softer" (L1161), "can likely" -> "will likely be able to" (L1163), "will possibly" -> "could possibly" (L1170). (5) **Story logic**: Birimo's "Time's up!" at 1:50 of a two-minute limit (L1161) is now "Done!". Facts checked: about one third of food is lost or wasted, about eight billion people (nine zeros after the eight, no s on billion), one billion = 9 zeros, ten million = 7 zeros, a quarter = one out of four, and two thirds takes an s. All of these are right. Night animals (owls, moths, bats) and darkness, and sensor lights, are right too. The garden-debate sides match L1151-1155 (Kiko and Birimo are the no team: "gardens take a lot of time"). In L1161 the rehearsal times agree (3:20, then 1:50), and in L1163 1:55 appears in both the teacherScript and the conversation. The wrap-up partners are unchanged. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 215-234 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1161 Preparing for the Conference
- A: Orbit's tips list (one main idea, key words, roles, stopwatch, nerves) is a real classroom checklist, and Haru cutting his lunch-tray story is a nice touch. In the teacherScript, "Haru looks worried. Two minutes? I could talk ..." has no "says". Birimo's "Ding! Time's up! One minute and fifty seconds. Perfect!" contradicts itself: the team finished early.
- B: seem / might / probably are well placed (probably = about 80% sure, with "show me eight fingers"). "These words are kinder than always or definitely" mislabels hedging: it is softer and more careful, not kinder. The JA やさしい いいかた fits "softer".
- C: In seg6 the voice reads "Only two minutes? I have so much to say ..." as me. Same "Time's up" flag.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg6 -> "... and says," (A+C). seg10 "kinder" -> "softer" (A+B). Line 8 "Time's up!" -> "Done!" with JA おわり！ (objective, story).

#### L1162 Opening Speech
- A: Kiko's speech is a believable kids'-conference opener, and the penguin card on Antarctica's empty chair is charming. seg8 "Then the topic. Today, we'll talk about, and the topic." is not a sentence. "Delegates from every continent ... Antarctica's chair is still empty" is a small tension, but "every continent" is the W39 framing (L1141 core "Children from every continent sit at this table."), so it is kept.
- B: The welcome -> topic -> thanks order is clear. appear / may / perhaps are fine. Kiko's "I may have spoken a bit too fast" after Haru's "you didn't rush" is natural self-doubt, so it is kept.
- C: In seg6 "Riko smiles. Just look at a friendly face, like mine!" and in seg7 "Kiko begins. Welcome, everyone." the voice welcomes everyone itself. seg8 is hard to read aloud.
- Decision: seg6 -> "Riko smiles and says," and seg7 -> "Kiko stands up and says," with JA 立って (A+C). seg8 -> "Then the topic. Today, we'll talk about food waste." with JA to match (A+C).

#### L1163 Presenting Our Proposal
- A: The proposal text (what can and can't go on the shelf, posters, song) is concrete and safe (no open food, no fridge food). Haru's "Almost every school can likely have one." is clumsy. "closed snack" could be "unopened snack", but that is a single flag (B prefers the simpler word), so it is kept.
- B: We propose that starts here, as the order rule needs. "every school has" is consistent across the lesson (US kid usage, fine). likely / possibly / usually are explained well. "can likely" breaks the lesson's own model, "It will likely save food".
- C: seg6 "Riko speaks first. We propose that ..." has no "says". seg8 "Then, This will help, and the good result." reads as a fragment.
- Decision: seg6 -> "She says," (A+C). seg8 -> "Then say the good result with This will help." (A+C). Line 6 -> "will likely be able to have one" (A+B).

#### L1164 Questions from Other Countries
- A: The Q&A notes (helpers each week with a teacher, Friday to the school kitchen, check the dates) are practical. Birimo's whispered "I'll guard the bananas day and night!" is fun. The title says "Countries" while the delegates stand for continents, but the title is test-pinned.
- B: "That's a great point. However, ~" is taught as praise, then a worry, which is good. generally / almost / about are correct ("About one third of food is wasted").
- C: seg6 "Birimo is playing Amina. I love your idea." has no "says". In seg7, "Kiko worries. What if ..." has no "says", and "And on Friday? The helpers give ..." has no speakers at all (it is Birimo as Mateo, then Riko). seg8 "Then, However, and your worry, kindly." reads as a fragment.
- Decision: seg6 -> "He says," (A+C). seg7 -> "Kiko worries and asks," / "Birimo asks, What happens on Friday? Riko says, ..." with JA speakers (A+C). seg8 -> "Then say However, and share your worry kindly." (A+C).

#### L1165 Reaching an Agreement
- A: The Round Table Promise and Birimo's smiley-face signature are lovely. The Lantern of Agreement lights at the block end, as pinned. "Signed, the children of every continent" is the same W39 framing as L1141, so it is kept.
- B: The review words are right (might, probably, On the OTHer hand + pause, eco + system). Therefore is used after L1150. There are 10 conversation lines, which is allowed at a block end.
- C: In seg6, "Haru sighs. That's hard." has no "says". In seg7, "Kiko laughs. Nice try, Birimo, ..." has no "says", and "Of course! Here's my signature." has no speaker.
- Decision: seg6 and seg7 -> "and says," and "Birimo says, Of course!" with JA ビリモ (A+C).

#### L1166 Reading Different Views
- A: The two articles are balanced and fair (energy, stars and night animals vs. safety, steps, bikes and older people). "Both writers care about our town" models respect.
- B: The facts are right (moths, owls and bats are affected by artificial light). Ten million = seven zeros is right. We believe that appears after L1152. The seg11 JA "小さな かわりかたに 見えるかも" is unnatural for "a small change".
- C: seg6 "Riko reads both articles. Article A and article B ..." and seg7 "Haru nods. Fair enough!" have no "says". In seg8, "I agree with parts of both." is read as me.
- Decision: seg6 and seg7 -> "and says," and seg8 -> "Kiko says," (A+C). seg11 JA -> 小さな へんかに (objective JA).

#### L1167 My Global Opinion Essay
- A: Riko's essay (on the one hand / on the other hand, Therefore, sensor lights as a middle way) is a strong model. Kiko's opposite but fair essay is a good message. TPR "Walk by and switch on the sensor light!" is a little odd, because a sensor light turns itself on. It is a single flag and a learning item, so it is kept.
- B: The two thirds s rule and "conserve, drop the e" are right. "add ation" uses a bare letter group.
- C: In seg6, "Because it's fair. If I show ..." (Riko's answer) has no speaker. In seg8, "What's your opinion? I think safety comes first." makes me say Kiko's opinion. "ation" may be misread by TTS.
- Decision: seg6 -> "Riko says," and seg8 -> "I ask, ... Kiko says, ..." with JA speakers (A+C). seg9 and the teacherScript -> "a, t, i, o, n" (B+C).

#### L1168 A Message to the World
- A: The message is hopeful and concrete (wells, library buses, beach clean-ups, and the children's own promises), and Birimo's P.S. joke is good. "As a result, the whole table seems to hum softly" is a loose cause (a single flag; it is magic), so it is kept. "from every continent" is the W39 framing, kept.
- B: About eight billion people is right. "nine zeros after the eight, no s on billion" is right. "If everyone helps a little, our planet will likely become ..." is a first conditional, which is fine. ほんとうの is kept in goalJa.
- C: seg7 "Riko laughs. Maybe not! What should we add? ..." has no "says", and the question is Haru's (conversation line 5). seg8 "Kiko adds a promise. We will ..." has no "says". "add al" is read as the name Al.
- Decision: seg7 -> "Riko laughs and says, ... Haru asks, ..." with JA ハルくん (A+C). seg8 -> "She says," (A+C). seg9 and the teacherScript -> "a, l" (B+C).

#### L1169 World Round Table Review
- A: The goodbye walk and the thank-you card are warm, and every child's story matches the earlier lessons (Amina's well, Priya's library bus, Nadia's lights and stars, Sam's shop, Mateo's uncle's carrots, Liam's beach). The garden debate memory matches L1151-1155. "we usually thought there was only one right answer" is a little forced (to fit the phonics word), but it is grammatical, so it is kept.
- B: The review words are right (a quarter = one out of four, In conCLUsion + pause). The bare "ed" in "plus ed" reads as the name Ed, not the -ed ending.
- C: seg7 "Kiko smiles. Your reasons were strong, too." has no "says". In seg8, "I learned that ..." and "It was a pleasure." have no speakers, so they are read as mine.
- Decision: seg7 -> "and says," and seg8 -> "Haru says," / "They say," with JA speakers (A+C). seg9 and the teacherScript -> "e, d" (B+C, as in L1141-1150).

#### L1170 World 39 Grand Review
- A: The closing ceremony, the Lantern of Hope, the beam up to the star and "That star shows where every journey began" set up W40 well. In Orbit's closing words, "Some problems will possibly take many years" is unnatural. The wrap-up has "Next, go and talk with Riko! Next is World forty, ...", two "Next"s in a row.
- B: "will possibly" mixes a sure word with a hedge, and "could possibly" is the form taught. One billion = nine zeros is right. The speed TPR "Look! Stop! Come here! Go!" stays first. There are 10 conversation lines (block end).
- C: In seg7, "Then everyone holds hands. Together, we can ..." has no "says". The double "Next" sounds like a slip.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "could possibly" (A+B). seg7 -> "holds hands and says," with JA つないで いうよ (A+C). seg19 second "Next is" -> "Then comes" (A+C). The partner line is unchanged.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Quotes with no "says" / answers with no speaker**: this is the main pattern in W39 Talk segments (seg6-8): "X smiles / sighs / laughs / nods / worries / begins. <quote>", and "Question? Answer." where the answer belongs to a child. Grep say for `\. (Kiko|Riko|Haru|Birimo) (smiles|laughs|sighs|nods|gasps|worries|begins)\. ` and for "? I learned / I agree / I think" with no speaker. It probably recurs in L1171-1200 too.
- **Frame sentences read aloud** ("Then, However, and your worry", "Today, we'll talk about, and the topic"): these "Here's the point" segments use a comma-list of phrase + "and the ~" that TTS cannot phrase. Give a full model sentence, or use "say X".
- **Bare letter groups**: I followed the L1141-1150 group (a, t, i, o, n / a, l / e, d; "En" and "Re" kept). Other review lessons that say "plus ation / plus al / plus ed" should match.
- **"every continent" vs Antarctica's empty chair** (L1162, L1165, L1168): this is kept because it is the pinned L1141 core framing ("Children from every continent sit at this table."). If the lead wants it fixed, it needs a world-wide decision, not a group fix.
- Kept on purpose: "closed snack(s)" (L1163-1164; simple for learners), TPR "Walk by and switch on the sensor light!" (L1167), "As a result, the whole table seems to hum softly" (L1168), "we usually thought" (L1169), "four weeks ago" (L1170; no timeline conflict found), the test-pinned title "Questions from Other Countries", "The Lantern of ~ lit up!", Birimo's "Time's up for debating" in L1169 (there the debate really is over), the seg7 JA "いい ちょうせんね" for "Nice try" in L1165, and the L1165 JA ビーチ / 海岸 variation.

### Run 9 review notes: L1171-1180 (world 40, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters, OK, "In our first year"). There are 59 patches. Every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "since I started" first appears in L1173 and "My advice is" in L1179, and no later form (I hope to, By the time I'm, couldn't have done it without, had been watching, Birimo's secret, later ray names) appears here. order.mjs prints nothing. Themes: (1) **Quotes with no speaker** in say / teacherScript (L1171 Celeste's "I'm Celeste", L1176 / L1179 / L1178 answers, L1180 Haru's core line). The voice read the character's words as mine, so I added "She says," / "Riko says," / "and says,". (2) **Bare letter groups in phonics** that TTS misreads: "oa / ow" (L1172), "ir / ur / er / ph" (L1173), "ai / ow / ph" (L1175), "ful" (L1177, L1180), "ly" (L1178), "ion" (L1179, said "eye-on"), "ea" (L1180). They are now spelled as letters ("o, w", "f, u, l"), as the say lines and L1131-1140 already do. The wrap-up lists "dis, ful and less" / "ness, ly and ment" / "ion, able and im" now name the real words. (3) **"lit up!" after present-tense narration** in the L1175 / L1180 teacherScript now has the speaker from the conversation (Riko / Kiko shouts). (4) **Facts and wording**: L1177 "children from every continent" -> "from all over the world" (Antarctica's chair is empty). L1176 "preview our folders" -> "flip through" (preview = look before). L1175 Haru's "Wait, really?" doubted a true line. L1178 "thumbs-ups". L1180 the heteronym "Bow." -> "Bow, like wow! ... Take a bow!". (5) **JA**: the tipJa "happy ＝ かなしい" / "like ＝ きらう" read as if the base word meant the opposite (now →). L1171 ベルみたいに なる, L1174 past すきだった, L1177 1 年生 -> 1 年目 (first year of English, not first grade). Facts checked: the timeline years in L1175 match the 200-lesson year plan in docs/curriculum-6year.md §5-1 (Kingdom of English W12 = year 2 and Birimo found a new map there; Ruins of Memory W16 = year 3; Amber Library W22 and Theater of Echoes W26 = year 4; Lighthouse of Ideas W29 and City of Tomorrow W30 = year 5; W36-39 = year 6). The recipe card from the Bustling Market (W14) is about four years old. Kiko gave the opening speech at the Round Table (W39). Ember, Skye, Nib, Lumi and Orbit are real guides. The six golden pages match W37. Only the Ray of Memories (L1175) and the Ray of Growth (L1180) appear, in their own lessons. The partners in the wrap-ups are unchanged. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 217-230 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1171 The Star of Beginnings
- A: The stairway of light, the silver grass and Celeste's welcome sign are a warm finale opening. Celeste smiling at Birimo is good foreshadowing for L1195. In the teacherScript, "Her voice sounds like a soft bell. Welcome, friends. I'm Celeste ..." and "Birimo blinks. Have we met before?" have no speaker. In seg6, "Haru asks, Who are you? I'm Celeste, ..." has no speaker for the answer.
- B: Began = the past of begin is clear. The ai / ay / ea review is right. The seg0 JA "ベルみたいに なるの" reads as "becomes like a bell".
- C: The voice says "I'm Celeste, the keeper" as me in seg6 and the teacherScript. "Bow to Celeste!" is fine in context.
- Decision: "She says, Welcome, friends." and "Birimo blinks and asks, ..." in the teacherScript (A+C). seg6 -> "She says, I'm Celeste, ..." with JA セレスト「…」 (A+C). seg0 JA -> ベルみたいな 音が するの (objective JA).

#### L1172 Back to the Forest
- A: The memory page matches the very first lessons (Hello! I'm Haru., Kiko's Bye-bye, sleepy Birimo). "Then Ms. Emma taught four magic words" is the brief's keeper. In seg8, "We said tells the old words" is unclear. In seg16 my "first English word" is Hello (single flag, kept).
- B: Same seg8 flag. ee / oa / ow are right.
- C: The teacherScript "but oa goes in the middle and ow goes at the end" has bare oa / ow (ow = ouch). "Birimo covers his face with his paws. I was very young then!" reads fine with the action cue (kept).
- Decision: seg8 -> "We said tells what we said back then." (A+B). The teacherScript -> "o, a ... o, w" (B+C).

#### L1173 I've Learned So Much
- A: Kiko's can-do list is a lovely six-year summary, and "mistakes help me learn" is a good message. Haru's "No way!" is surprise at Riko's "couldn't even spell cat", which is natural here (kept).
- B: The present perfect and since I started are explained simply. igh / ir / ph are right.
- C: The teacherScript has bare "ir sounds just like ur and er" (er = a hesitation sound) and "ph sounds like f".
- Decision: the teacherScript -> "i, r sounds just like u, r and e, r" and "p, h sounds like f" (B+C).

#### L1174 My Favorite World
- A: Riko's card (Nib, golden pages, storytelling night) matches W37. Kiko's "Aww, I loved them too." is a little odd for fish (single flag, kept). Birimo's dumplings joke is fun.
- B: favorite + because + reason is clear. The silent letters k / w / b are right. The seg15 JA "いちばん すき" is present, but "I liked ... best" is past.
- C: Reads well. "Wrong, like Birimo's joke" ties the word to the story.
- Decision: seg15 JA -> すきだったよ (objective JA).

#### L1175 Journey Timeline
- A: The ribbon timeline is a nice hands-on review. In conversation line 6, Haru's "Wait, really?" answers Kiko's true line about the conference, as if he doubts it. In the teacherScript, "The Ray of Memories lit up!" comes after present-tense narration with no speaker, and Celeste's and Birimo's lines have no speaker.
- B: The years match the year plan (see the summary). The "lit up" flag is the same: it is a character's exclamation, so it needs a speaker. "Step to the side, one by one" is slightly odd (single flag, kept).
- C: The teacherScript phonics has bare "ai", "ow" and "ph". The same speaker flags.
- Decision: line 6 and the seg8 slide -> "Wait, what's that? Look! ..." with JA え、あれ なに？ (A+B). The teacherScript -> "Riko shouts, The Ray of Memories lit up! Celeste claps her tiny hands and says, ..." and "Birimo counts on his paws and says, ..." (A+C). Letters spelled (B+C).

#### L1176 My Best Work
- A: The portfolio and Riko's Moon Fox label are a strong model. "We all preview our folders" misuses preview (look at something before it happens). In seg6 and seg7, Riko's lines have no speaker.
- B: Same preview flag, and it is the phonics word of the day, so it should be used right. The tipJa "happy ＝ かなしい" and "write ＝ 書きなおす" read as if the base words had those meanings. The portfolio（作品集）hint is the test-allowed JA in this lesson.
- C: In seg7, "Because I rewrote the ending ..." is read as my answer. "un / re / pre" are said as syllables; TTS reads them well enough (kept).
- Decision: "flip through our folders" (A+B). seg6 "... and says, This is the story ..." and seg7 "Riko says, Because ..." with JA リコちゃん「…」 (A+C). tipJa uses → for the result (objective JA).

#### L1177 Me Then, Me Now
- A: Kiko's chart is honest and kind ("I believe in myself now"). "giving the opening speech in front of children from every continent" is wrong: Antarctica's chair is empty. seg11 "Now chin up. Now stand tall" is clumsy. The seg20 "use used to" is clumsy aloud.
- B: used to = true before, not now is right. dis / ful / less are right, but the tipJa "like ＝ きらう" reads as like = dislike. tpr[0].ja / seg11 JA say 1 年生 (first grade) for "first-year self" (the first year of English).
- C: Bare "care plus ful" in the teacherScript. The list "dis, ful and less" in seg20.
- Decision: "from all over the world" (objective fact). "Careful is care plus f, u, l" (B+C). seg11 -> "Chin up! Now stand tall and proud!" (A+C). seg20 -> "talk about what you used to do, and build words like dislike, careful and careless." with JA to match (A+C). 1 年生 -> 1 年目 and tipJa → (objective JA).

#### L1178 What Was the Hardest Part?
- A: Honest hard parts (goed / eated, R and L, waiting) and Kiko's diary are very real. "Haru gave me thumbs-ups" is not a natural plural. In seg7, "My friends did." has no speaker. Celeste's line in the teacherScript has no speaker.
- B: -ness / -ment make nouns and -ly tells how: right. Same thumbs-up flag.
- C: Bare "ly" (read as lie) in seg9 and the teacherScript. The list "ness, ly and ment" in seg20. "I read too quickly" is a heteronym, but the past context is clear (kept).
- Decision: "gave me a thumbs-up" (A+B). "Celeste shines warmly and says, ..." and seg7 "Kiko says, My friends did." with JA キコちゃん「…」 (A+C). "l, y" in seg9 and the teacherScript, and seg20 names kindness, quickly and movement (B+C).

#### L1179 Advice for New Learners
- A: New first-year children in the Forest is a beautiful full circle, and the advice is warm and true. In seg7, Riko's "Because a little every day ..." has no speaker.
- B: "My advice is to + verb" starts here as planned. im before p is right. "ion" said as a word teaches the wrong sound (the ending is "shun").
- C: Bare "ion" in seg9 and the teacherScript (eye-on). The list "ion, able and im" in seg20.
- Decision: seg7 "Riko says, Because ..." with JA リコちゃん「…」 (A+C). "i, o, n" in seg9 and the teacherScript, and seg20 names invention, comfortable and impossible (B+C).

#### L1180 Portfolio Presentation
- A: Haru's introduction shows real growth (one word "fish" -> a science talk). Birimo's flat-to-fluffy pancakes are a perfect joke. Haru's core line "Let me show you my portfolio" and his seg7 line have no speaker. "The Ray of Growth lit up!" and Celeste's line in the teacherScript have no speaker.
- B: Same speaker flags. The review words are right. "Bow." alone is a heteronym (bow = ribbon).
- C: Bare "ful" and "ea". A lone "Bow." may be read as boh.
- Decision: the teacherScript "smiles, opens his folder and says, ...", "Kiko shouts, The Ray of Growth lit up! Celeste rings like a bell and says, ..." and "f, u, l" / "e, a" (A+C, B+C). seg6 / seg7 "and says," with seg7 JA 発表を 見せるよ (A+C). seg9 "f, u, l" (B+C). seg4 -> "Bow, like wow! Bend forward politely, like this. Take a bow!" with JA (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Quotes with no speaker** are common in W40 ("Celeste claps her tiny hands. You ...", "Kiko asks, ...? Because ...", "Haru asks, ...? My friends did."). Grep say / teacherScript in L1181-1200 for "? Because", "? I'm", "? My" and Celeste's lines after an action.
- **Bare letter groups in W40 phonics**: the say lines mostly spell letters ("a i", "o w"), but the teacherScript phonics sentences use bare groups ("Rain has ai", "ow", "ph", "ea", "ful", "ly", "ion"). Check L1181-1184 roots (spect, struct, port, dict, vis, aud) and the reviews L1185, L1190, L1195-1200 the same way. Real-word roots that are also words (port) are fine.
- **"<Ray> lit up!" after present-tense narration**: give it a speaker in the L1185 / 1190 / 1195 / 1200 teacherScripts too.
- **"Bow!" alone** stays in the L1171 / L1180 speed challenges (inside a command list; TPR en "Bow to Celeste!" / "Bow and say, Thank you!" give context). Watch L1181 / L1191 / L1200.
- **L1141 core "Children from every continent sit at this table."** is test-pinned (tests/curriculum.test.ts:4519), but Antarctica's chair is empty. I fixed only my L1177 sentence. The core cannot change without a test change; reported.
- Kept on purpose: "un / re / pre / dis / ness / ment" said as syllables (TTS reads them as sounds); "No way!" as surprise in L1173; my "I said, Hello!" in L1172 seg16; "Aww" lines; "Then Ms. Emma taught four magic words" (brief keeper); "But Ms. Emma said, Being nervous means you care." in Kiko's diary (brief keeper); the conversation-line JA エマ先生 when children speak to me.

### Run 9 review notes: L1181-1190 (world 40, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 70 patches, and every lesson has at least one. There is one learning-item change, a US-policy fix: L1187 school.expressions[1].en "... I'd like to study sea animals at university." -> "... at a university." Every field that repeats it was updated too (targetExpressions[1], recap, conversation line 6, teacherScript, seg1/8/16/21 slides, seg8 and seg16 say/subtitleEn). No test or src line references it: I grepped tests/ and src/lib/curriculum/ for "at university" and found nothing, and the L1187 core is untouched. The grammar order is clean. order.mjs prints nothing, "I hope to" first appears in L1186, "By the time I'm" first appears in L1187, and no ray name appears early. Themes: (1) **Quotes with no "says" / "asks"** (about 20 places in L1181-1190): the characters' interview lines, Birimo's examiner questions and the answers came right after an action sentence, so the voice read them as mine. Adding "and says," / "asks," fixes them. (2) **Story continuity**: in L1183 the teacherScript gives "That's a good point. I didn't think about rainy days." to Riko, but in the conversation and seg7 Kiko says it. In L1185 the teacherScript gives "Being brave doesn't mean ..." to Celeste, but seg9 gives it to Birimo. (3) **JA name**: セレステ (6 subtitles in L1181 and L1186-1189) -> セレスト, the form used 78 times and in the test name. (4) **US usage**: "heavy shopping" -> "heavy groceries" (L1183), "at university" -> "at a university" (L1187). (5) **Pen-pal safety** (L1188): pen pals come up, so the word segment now says to find one with a grown-up's help. Facts checked: the 1185 and 1190 ray names and the ray counts (two, three and four rays shining) are right. "Six years ago, I could only say hello" matches the first lesson. Lanternfish do live in the deep sea, and English is the language pilots use with the control tower. There are no real test names; "In many interview tests" is generic. Every starter keeps its "Let's pretend I'm" frame, and the wrap-up partners are unchanged. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 215-228 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1181 Interview Practice 1
- A: The light room, the knock / walk in / sit / bow sequence and "Examiners want to hear your ideas, not perfect English" are realistic and kind. In seg3, "You tell people about you." is unnatural. The examiner's "Thank you." in the starter after the child sits is a little odd, but real examiners do say it. Kept.
- B: The privacy line (story self, nickname) is clear. The roots spect / struct / dict are right. seg3: same flag; the core says "about yourself". "I like gentle songs, because ..." has a comma before because, but that is house style across W39-40.
- C: In seg8, "Birimo jumps in. What kind of songs do you like?" has no "asks", so the question reads as mine. The bare roots "spect", "struct" and "dict" are pronounceable, and the W29-W34 precedent is "Spect means look.", so they stay.
- Decision: seg3 -> "You tell people about yourself." (A+B). seg8 -> "Birimo jumps in and asks, ..." (A+C). seg0 JA セレステ -> セレスト (objective JA).

#### L1182 Interview Practice 2
- A: The park picture card and the model answer (big picture, then each person, then one idea of your own) are a good interview strategy. Birimo staring at the ice cream cart is fun. Conversation line 7, "There are and the ing form make your answers clear.", is hard to parse.
- B: Same line 7 flag. The present continuous for pictures is explained well, and the quiz (walks / is walking) targets it. In seg7, "And the girl? She's reading a book." has no speakers, so I seem to answer my own question.
- C: In seg6, seg7 and seg8, Birimo's questions and Haru's answer have no speaker. Same in the teacherScript: "Then Birimo grabs the picture card. I'm playing the examiner!" The bare "ing" is read fine ("the ing form"), so it stays.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg6 / seg8 -> "and says," / "and asks," and seg7 -> "Birimo asks, And the girl? Haru says, ..." with the JA to match (A+C). Line 7 -> "Using There are and the ing form makes your answers clear." (A+B).

#### L1183 Interview Practice 3
- A: The opinion card with Answer A and Answer B ("both answers are good answers") is excellent. "heavy shopping" is British (US: groceries).
- B: The two reasons with first / second are clear. The teacherScript has Riko say "That's a good point. I didn't think about rainy days.", but in conversation line 5 and seg7 Kiko says it (Riko is the one being answered). Riko's "there are fewer traffic jams" as a reason for more cyclists is loose logic (it reads as a result). Single flag, kept.
- C: In the teacherScript, "Kiko looks worried. What if I give the wrong answer? I smile." has no "asks", so "I" reads as me.
- Decision: "Kiko looks worried and asks, ..." (A+C). "Riko nods. That's a good point." -> "Kiko nods and says, ..." (objective, story). "heavy shopping" -> "heavy groceries" (objective, US policy).

#### L1184 Interview Practice 4
- A: The pancake-mumbling examiner is a funny, memorable way to teach "Could you say that again, please?". "Let me see…" stays (a keeper). In seg6, "Birimo has pancake in the mouth." is unnatural.
- B: Same seg6 flag. The tips (do not panic, thinking time, I'm not sure but I think, do not stay silent) are sound interview advice. tele / graph / geo are right (phone = sound).
- C: In seg6, seg7 and seg8, Birimo's mumbled question, "Oops! What's your favorite season ...?" and the tricky animal question have no speaker, so they read as mine.
- Decision: seg6 -> "Birimo's mouth is full of pancake. He mumbles, ..." (A+B+C). seg7 / seg8 -> "Birimo says, Oops! ..." / "Birimo asks, If you could ..." with the JA to match (A+C).

#### L1185 Mock Interview
- A: The real-room setup, Kiko's shaking hands and "Being nervous is normal" are warm. The Kite Day passage is a good read-aloud. seg21 has "Nervous is normal." In seg8 the JA has カイト・デー but the expression JA has たこの 日 (single flag, kept).
- B: The teacherScript says "Celeste says, Being brave doesn't mean you aren't nervous.", but seg9 has "Birimo says, Being brave ...", and conversation line 9 has Birimo say it right after "Celeste is ringing like a bell!" without saying it is hers. seg21 "Nervous is normal" uses an adjective as the subject. The Ray of Courage lights here, as the test needs, and three rays are shining.
- C: In seg7, "Kiko breathes. Thank you. I feel better now. I'm ready." has no "says", so it reads as mine. The line 9 JA "鈴みたいに なってる" reads as "becoming", not "ringing".
- Decision: seg9 -> "Celeste rings like a bell and says, Being brave ..." with the JA to match. Line 9 -> "... She says being brave doesn't mean ..." with JA 鳴ってる / ことなんだって, and the two slides that show line 9 updated (objective, story). seg7 -> "Kiko breathes and says, ..." (A+C). seg21 -> "Being nervous is normal." (A+B).

#### L1186 I Hope to…
- A: The wishes rising as lights from the Pool of Starlight and the hope list are lovely. Birimo's "No way! Can I come?" is natural US excitement. The JA うそ！ fits, and L1089's "No way" was different (it read as a refusal), so it stays.
- B: I hope to starts here, as the test needs. "We use I hope to when we'd really like to do something in the future" is clear. The speech-pause stress (aGO, EVeryone, SIX) is right.
- C: In seg7, "Kiko asks, Why ...? Because I want to learn ..." has no speaker for Riko's answer. The "pause" read aloud is the W36-38 decision, so it stays.
- Decision: seg7 -> "... Riko? Riko says, Because ..." with the JA to match (A+C). seg0 JA セレステ -> セレスト (objective JA).

#### L1187 By the Time I'm Twenty
- A: The fake mustache and the future plan are fun. "That suits you." is natural. "study sea animals at university" is British. US says "at a university" or "in college".
- B: By the time I'm is taught for understanding only ("You don't need to use it yourself yet"). That is good scaffolding for B1+. "Dreams can grow and change" is a good message. Same "at university" flag.
- C: The teacherScript has "Then Riko closes her eyes and smiles. By the time I'm twenty, ..." and "Birimo takes off his mustache. I like being me now, too.", and seg6 has "Birimo puts on a fake mustache. I'm playing ..." and "Kiko laughs. It's very fancy!". None of them has "says", so all read as mine.
- Decision: "at university" -> "at a university" in all 13 fields (objective, US policy; learning item, no test reference). "says" added in the teacherScript (2) and seg6 (2) (A+C). seg0 JA セレステ -> セレスト.

#### L1188 English and My Future
- A: The moving starlight pictures (scientist, doctor, pilot, translator) and "English isn't only for work" are good. "a pilot talking to the airport" is vague. Pilots talk to the control tower. Pen pals come up (the reading, seg5 and the TPR "Type a letter to a pen pal!") with no safety word.
- B: Same pen-pal flag. A child at this age should find a pen pal through a grown-up or school. "I want to use English to + verb" and the to / for quiz are clear. The stress in FUture / YOU / WHAT is right.
- C: In seg6, "Riko points. Look! A translator ..." has no "says".
- Decision: seg6 -> "Riko points and says, Look!" (A+C). seg5 adds "Find one with a grown-up's help." with JA おうちの 人と いっしょに さがしてね (A+B). The teacherScript says "talking to the control tower" (A+B). seg0 JA セレステ -> セレスト.

#### L1189 A Letter to Future Me
- A: Haru's letter, which looks back six years to "I could only say hello" (that matches the first lesson), and Birimo's one-line letter are moving and funny. In seg18, "Are you still, and then your hobby?" is an instruction written as a question.
- B: The letter frame (question, memory, kind advice) is clear. The stress in FINish / BACK / toGETHer is right.
- C: The seg18 "?" makes the voice rise as if it were asking the child something.
- Decision: seg18 "?" -> "." (A+C). seg0 and seg21 JA セレステ -> セレスト (objective JA).

#### L1190 Future Dreams Fair
- A: The booths and the pancake chef on a spaceship are delightful, and "Dreams can change, and that's OK" is a strong ending for the block. Lanternfish do live in the deep sea, and Deep Sea City is a back-reference to W35-36.
- B: The Ray of Dreams lights here, as the test needs, and four rays are shining. The core frame "In the future, I'd like to ~ because ~" is right. The seg4 JA "たいよう" (大洋) is read by kids as 太陽 (the sun).
- C: In seg8, "Haru laughs. Do pancakes float in space?" has no "asks".
- Decision: seg8 -> "Haru laughs and asks, ..." (A+C). seg4 JA たいよう -> うみ (objective JA; same as W31-32).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Quotes after an action sentence with no "says"**: "X jumps in. <quote>", "X points. <quote>", "X laughs. <quote>", "X breathes. <quote>" and "And the girl? <answer>" are common in W40 say fields and teacherScripts. Grep say / teacherScript for `\. (Look|Oops|Thank you|Do |What |Can |I'm |It's )` after a character action in L1171-1180 and L1191-1200.
- **セレステ vs セレスト**: 6 subtitles in world-40 used セレステ (all in this group, all now fixed). The other groups should be clean, but grep once after the merge.
- **"... lit up!" in present-tense narration** (L1185 / L1190 teacherScript: "begins to shine ... The Ray of Courage lit up!"): this mixes tenses, but "The Ray of Memories lit up!" is a W40 expression (L1175) and the same exclamation is used in every ray scene. Kept for consistency. Fix it in all six ray lessons or in none.
- **at university / shopping (= groceries)**: British forms that may appear in other W40 future-plan lessons (L1191-1200 speeches recap Haru's sea-animal plan).
- **Pen pals and letters**: if L1191-1200 mention pen pals or writing to strangers, add the grown-up line.
- Kept on purpose: the bare roots spect / struct / dict / rupt / ject / port / vis / scrib / aud / tele / graph / geo / re (pronounceable, and the W29-W34 precedent "Spect means look."); the bare "ing"; "Let me see…"; the "pause" read aloud and the capital-letter stress; Birimo's "No way! Can I come?" (L1186) and Haru's "No way! Do pancakes float in space?" (L1190), both excitement, not refusal; the comma before because; the examiner's "Thank you." in the L1181 starter; the L1185 JA カイト・デー / たこの 日 variation; Riko's "fewer traffic jams" reason (L1183); "Ms. Emma" inside Haru's letter, the hope list and "A note from Ms. Emma" (texts the children wrote).

### Run 9 review notes: L1191-1200 (world 40, Stage 5)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 76 patches (say and subtitleEn count as two). Every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item changes: school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en and phonics words are untouched, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: couldn't have done it without first appears in L1191 and had been watching in L1195 (order.mjs prints nothing). "Birimo's secret" and "Ray of Thanks" / "Ray of Beginnings" do not appear early. Themes: (1) **Quotes with no "says"** (the biggest one; 18 places in L1192-1200): "Riko points. Orbit, Puff ...", "Haru smiles. The places ...", "Birimo yawns. I said, ..." and answers with no speaker made the voice read the child's words as mine. I added "and says," / "X says," and the JA name. (2) **Bare sound spellings for TTS**: "Say er" / "Work has er" / "Hard has ar" (L1194, L1195) now use real words (the sound in her, the sound in car). "ay", "ness", "ir / er / ur" and "Im" (L1196-1198) are now spelled as letters. In L1199, "Make FU strong" (TTS may read F, U, which is rude in the US) is now "Make the start of future strong". The capital-stress "In the FUture" stays. (3) **Heteronyms**: "Lead has L" (led / leed) in L1191 now says "Lead, as in lead the way", and "Tear." in L1192 now says "Tear, as in teardrop". (4) **Meaning / story**: L1191's "if you were not here, I couldn't do it" explanation was in the wrong time. In L1192's teacherScript, "we all shout, Surprise!" had me surprising myself. "Once upon a time" was called "the oldest words" in L1197. In L1198, "Priya, who lives on another continent" may be false (both may be from Asia). (5) **JA**: L1195 "やさしく なって" (reads as "became kind") and L1196 "こうかい した" (reads as "regretted"). Facts checked: the guide names in L1191's letters (Ember on the Island of Fire, Skye, Olive, Lumi and Dr. Vale, Nib, Puff and Grandma Rowan, Orbit) and the Kingdom of English map (L1197; it matches L1172) are all in earlier worlds. The ray order is right (4 rays in L1191, 5 in L1195, 6 in L1200). The partners in the wrap-ups are unchanged. The check script passes with ALL OK. Lessons run 215-235 s with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L1191 Thank You, Everyone
- A: The memories (Riko held Kiko's hand, Kiko read with Haru) and the letters to the old guides are warm. seg4 "A glass pot with a lid" is odd in the US (a pot is for cooking). Ms. Emma's "That's lovely." in conversation line 7 is fine in US speech too (single flag, kept).
- B: couldn't have done it without starts here, as the order rule needs. seg7 explains it as "if you were not here, I couldn't do it", which is present or imagined time, not the past. It is also a conditional the course has not taught for this meaning. The minimal pair read / lead needs /liːd/.
- C: "Lead has L." alone may be read as "led" (the metal). "Vvv." reads OK.
- Decision: seg4 -> "A glass container with a lid on top." (A+B). seg7 -> "That means, I did it because you were there to help!" with JA あなたが いて くれたから できた (A+B). seg9 and teacherScript -> "Lead, as in lead the way, has L / starts with L" (B+C).

#### L1192 Thank You, Ms. Emma
- A: Birimo the pancake reporter and the card are lovely. seg0 "a little funny for me" does not mean embarrassed. In the teacherScript, "we all shout, Surprise!" is in my own first-person narration, but I am the one being surprised. seg19's quiz options are not parallel (to keep me busy / Birimo wants to sell) (single flag, kept).
- B: The JA for seg0 is てれくさい, which does not match "funny". "Tear" is a heteronym that learners also need to hear right.
- C: In seg6, "Leave it to me!" has no speaker. seg7 "Birimo holds a spoon ... Breaking news!" and seg9 "Then, Surprise, Ms. Emma!" have no speaker either, so the voice says "Surprise, Ms. Emma!" to itself. "Tear." alone may be read as "tare". "I read the card twice" in the teacherScript is fine either way (present-tense narration).
- Decision: seg0 -> "makes me a little shy" (A+B). seg5 -> "Tear, as in teardrop." (B+C). seg6 / seg7 / seg9 add "Birimo says," / "and says," / "everyone shouts," with JA ビリモ / みんなが (A+C). teacherScript -> "everyone jumps out and shouts, Surprise!" (objective, story).

#### L1193 Writing My Graduation Speech
- A: The vote, the outline and the first draft are a strong model. Birimo's pancake line is fun.
- B: The four-part outline (past, journey, thank-yous, future) matches the draft. she/see, fall/hall and hat/hut are good tricky pairs. In seg10, "like a big yawn" for /æ/ hints at a back "ah" more than a spread "a" (single flag, kept; the JA tip says 口を 大きく あける).
- C: In seg6, "Kiko's eyes go wide. Me? Wait, really?" has no speaker. "Maybe a small one" in seg8 could be me (kept).
- Decision: seg6 -> "Kiko's eyes go wide, and she says, Me? ..." (A+C).

#### L1194 Final Speech Rehearsal
- A: The starlight stage, Birimo "playing the audience, all of it", and the six tips are natural rehearsal language. The JA ひと休み for "pause" is the block-wide pause word (kept).
- B: The tips are correct (posture, slow down, pause after an important line, eye contact, it's OK to be nervous). world/word, walk/work and heard/hard are right.
- C: In seg6, "Oh no, I went way too fast" and in seg8, "How was that?" have no speaker. "Say er", "Work has er", "Heard has er" and "Hard has ar" are read as hesitation sounds or letters.
- Decision: seg6 -> "Then she sighs, Oh no, ..." with JA ためいき. seg8 -> "Kiko tries again and asks, ..." (A+C). seg9 / seg10 -> "the sound in her", "Heard starts like her", "the sound in car" (B+C).

#### L1195 Birimo's Secret
- A: Celeste's story of the tiny light is moving, and "My home is wherever my friends are!" is a perfect turn. "The Ray of Thanks lit up!" works as an exclamation (kept).
- B: had been watching starts here, as the order rule needs. "Birimo's secret" and Ray of Thanks are in the right lesson. In seg0, the JA "セレストが やさしく なって" reads as "Celeste became kind", not "rings softly".
- C: In seg6, "Haru gasps. Wait, really?" and in seg9, "Birimo thinks. I love this star" have no speaker. In seg10, "Say er" has the same problem as in L1194. The "Thanks to YOU" stress caps are intended.
- Decision: seg0 JA -> やさしく チリンと ひびいて (objective JA). seg6 / seg9 add "and says," / "then says," (A+C). seg10 -> "Say the sound in her, then L." (B+C).

#### L1196 Grand Review: Talk About Anything
- A: The topic wheel, the "playing catch" image and the follow-up-question tips are real US classroom free-talk language. Strawberry soup from the Bustling Market is a nice callback.
- B: The three steps (more than one sentence, follow-up question, reaction) are clear. In seg8, the JA "しまへ こうかい したの" in hiragana reads as 後悔 (regretted).
- C: In seg8, the answer "She sailed to an island ..." has no speaker. "Play ends with ay." is read as "aye".
- Decision: seg8 -> "Riko says, She sailed ..." with JA リコちゃん「… ふねで いったの！」 (A+C, objective JA). seg10 and teacherScript -> "a, y" (B+C).

#### L1197 Grand Review: Tell a Story
- A: Haru's "didn't we meet before we found the map?" is a great storytelling correction. "Once upon a time" called "the oldest words in storytelling" is not true.
- B: The beginning / middle (problem) / ending frame is taught well. The Kingdom of English matches the L1172 timeline. "kind plus ness makes a thing word" is fine for kids. "And she, round your lips." reads as if she is the subject.
- C: seg6 "Riko jumps up. Me, please!" and "Good point!", and seg7 "Haru adds the middle. The hard parts, like when I kept saying goed", have no speaker (the "I" sounds like me). A bare "ness" may be read as the name Ness.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "the most famous words" (A+B). seg6 / seg7 add "and says," / "Riko says," with JA リコちゃん (A+C). seg9 and teacherScript -> "n, e, s, s" (B+C). seg10 -> "And for she, round your lips." (A+C).

#### L1198 Grand Review: Share Your Opinion
- A: Riko's speech (opinion, two reasons, example, opinion again) is a model US opinion speech. Birimo's pancake reason turns into a real point. The JA しんじて います for "I believe that" is a little literal but OK (kept).
- B: In the speech, "Priya, who lives on another continent" may be false: Priya speaks for Asia at the Round Table, and Riko is a Japanese child, so both may be from Asia. In seg7, the JA "まるいテーブル" loses the name 円卓.
- C: In seg6 / seg7 / seg8, "Riko stands tall. I believe ...", "Sure. At the Round Table ..." and "Birimo stands up. I believe ...", plus the answer "Because when you share food ...", have no speaker. "ir / er / ur" and "Im means not" are read as words (I'm).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "who lives far away" (objective, fact). The speaker fixes in seg6 / seg7 / seg8 with JA リコちゃん / ビリモ and 円卓 (A+C, objective JA). seg9 and teacherScript -> "i, r", "e, r and u, r", "i, m" (B+C).

#### L1199 Graduation Eve
- A: The starry night, mixed feelings and the goodnight page are beautiful. Kiko's "Me neither." correctly answers "I can't believe ...". The TPR "Goodnight!" as one word is common (single flag, kept). "FU" alone would never be written in US kids' material.
- B: The mixed-feelings language ("a little sad, but excited, too") is clear. knee / preview / telephone / fall are right.
- C: In seg7, "Birimo yawns. I said, I'm sleepy." has no speaker. TTS may read "Make FU strong" as the letters F, U.
- Decision: seg7 -> "Birimo yawns and says, I remember! I said, ..." with JA (A+C). seg10 and teacherScript -> "the start of future" (A+C). The display "In the FUture" and the JA FU stay.

#### L1200 World 40 Grand Finale
- A: Kiko's speech, the guides returning, the four magic words and the blank map make a fitting finale. In the teacherScript, I lead the magic words, but in conversation line 4 and seg7 Kiko does. Both can happen (single flag, kept).
- B: The Ray of Beginnings appears only here. The speed TPR is pinned and unchanged. comfort + able and graph = write are right.
- C: In seg6, "Riko points. Orbit, Puff ..." and in seg8, "Haru smiles. The places will appear ..." have no speaker. The "ToGETHer" / "GETH" stress reads fine.
- Decision: seg6 / seg8 add "and says," (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Quotes with no "says"**: this is very common in W40 story segments ("X smiles. <quote>", "X points. <quote>", "X yawns. <quote>", a reply with no speaker after a question). grep say for `\. (Me|Wait|Sure|Because|Good point|Oh no)` and "X <verb>s\. [A-Z]" in L1171-1190. The teacherScript has the same pattern ("Birimo smiles shyly. I forgot!" L1195, "Birimo stands up proudly. I believe" L1198, "Haru smiles. This is not the end." L1200). I left those because TTS reads `say`.
- **Bare sound spellings**: "er", "ar", "ay", "ir", "ur", "Im", "ness" in say. W40 reviews (L1175, 1180, 1185, 1190) may have "Say er" or "ends with ay". Use real words (her, car) or spelled letters.
- **"FU" stress**: if another speech-pause drill isolates a stressed syllable that is a rude letter pair, describe it ("the start of future") and keep the capital form in the display.
- **lead / tear / read in the tricky-sounds drills**: give context for heteronyms ("lead, as in lead the way", "tear, as in teardrop").
- **"another continent" / where the children are from**: watch W39-W40 for claims that Riko / Haru / Kiko are on a different continent from Priya (Asia).
- Kept on purpose: "That's lovely." (L1191 line 7), "like a big yawn" (L1193), ひと休み for pause, しんじて います, "Goodnight!", "The Ray of Thanks / Beginnings lit up!", the capital stress "In the FUture" / "ToGETHer" / "Thanks to YOU", "goed" (L1197), and Ms. Emma in the cards and speeches ("Dear Ms. Emma", "Most of all, Ms. Emma taught us", "With love, Ms. Emma").

| 1142 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Children from every continent sit at this table. Today, it's lunchtime at the Hall of the Round Table. There is a long buffet with rice, bread, noodles, soup, salad and fruit from every continent. Birimo grabs the biggest plate he can find. He takes three rolls, two bowls of noodles, a mountain of rice and five pancakes. The mountain is taller than his head! He eats and eats, and then he leans back and holds his tummy. I can't eat another bite! Sam looks at all the leftovers and smiles kindly. He says, In my town, we learned something surprising. About one third of food is wasted. Say it with me. About one third of food is wasted. Well done! Birimo's face turns pink. Oops. Sorry, food! Orbit spins over to us and shows us a fact card. Let's read it. "Food Facts from the Round Table. Around the world, about one third of all the food we grow is never eaten. Some food goes bad on farms, some is thrown away at shops, and some is left on plates at home and at school. When food is wasted, the water, land and hard work that went into it are wasted, too. But here is the good news. People everywhere are finding ways to help. Many schools now let children take smaller servings and come back for more. Some families plan their meals and turn leftovers into new dishes, like fried rice or soup. Every bite we eat instead of throwing away makes a difference." How much food is wasted around the world? About one third. What can families do with leftovers? They can make new dishes, like fried rice or soup. Birimo takes a small plate this time and wraps up his extra rolls to share later. Now, phonics. More environment words are built from parts. Pollution comes from pollute. Drop the e and add ion. Recycle is re, which means again, plus cycle, which means circle. Conservation comes from conserve. Drop the e and add ation. | 1143 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, About one third of food is wasted. Today, Amina from Africa shares a story with the whole table. She holds up a photo of her town.
